```edgeAI/Claude-Nvidia Jetson project ideas for internship.txt
   713	File 3 of 7 — backend/models.py
   714	
   715	This defines the shape of data going in and out of your API. Nothing fancy, just Pydantic schemas.
   716	
   717	from pydantic import BaseModel
   718	from typing import Optional
   719	
   720	# --- Request bodies ---
   721	
   722	class QueryRequest(BaseModel):
   723	    question: str                    # the user's question from the frontend
   724	
   725	# --- Response bodies ---
   726	
   727	class Citation(BaseModel):
   728	    text: str                        # the actual chunk text from the handbook
   729	    source: str                      # which PDF file it came from
   730	    page: Optional[int] = None       # page number if available
   731	
   732	class QueryResponse(BaseModel):
   733	    answer: str                      # LLM's generated answer
   734	    citations: list[Citation]        # list of source chunks used
   735	    confidence: float                # similarity score of best retrieved chunk
   736	    found: bool                      # False if below confidence threshold
   737	
   738	class IngestResponse(BaseModel):
   739	    message: str                     # success/failure message
   740	    chunks_indexed: int              # how many chunks were created from the PDF
   741	    filename: str                    # the uploaded file's name
   742	
   743	Every endpoint in FastAPI will use these models — the frontend will always know exactly what shape of data to expect back.
   744	
   745	File 4 of 7 — backend/ingest.py
   746	
   747	This is the first core file. It takes a PDF, pulls out the text, cuts it into chunks, embeds each chunk into a vector, and saves the index to disk.
   748	
   749	import os
   750	import pickle
   751	import fitz                          # PyMuPDF — fitz is its internal name
   752	import faiss
   753	import numpy as np
   754	from rank_bm25 import BM25Okapi
   755	from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
   756	from dotenv import load_dotenv
   757	
   758	load_dotenv()
   759	
   760	EMBED_MODEL = os.getenv("EMBED_MODEL")
   761	INDEX_DIR   = os.getenv("INDEX_DIR")
   762	CHUNK_SIZE  = 200        # target tokens per chunk
   763	CHUNK_OVERLAP = 20       # overlap between consecutive chunks (in words)
   764	
   765	# Load embedding model once at module level so it
   766	# doesn't reload on every ingest call
   767	embedder = SentenceTransformer(EMBED_MODEL)
   768	
   769	def extract_text_from_pdf(pdf_path: str) -> list[dict]:
   770	    """
   771	    Opens a PDF and returns a list of dicts, one per page.
   772	    Each dict has 'text' and 'page' (1-indexed).
   773	    """
   774	    doc = fitz.open(pdf_path)
   775	    pages = []
   776	    for i, page in enumerate(doc):
   777	        text = page.get_text("text").strip()
   778	        if text:                     # skip blank pages
   779	            pages.append({"text": text, "page": i + 1})
   780	    doc.close()
   781	    return pages
   782	
   783	def chunk_text(pages: list[dict], source: str) -> list[dict]:
   784	    """
   785	    Splits page text into overlapping word-level chunks.
   786	    Returns list of dicts with 'text', 'source', 'page'.
   787	
   788	    Why word-level? Because token counts vary by model but
   789	    word counts are stable — 200 words ≈ 150–250 tokens,
   790	    which keeps chunks well within 1B's sweet spot.
   791	    """
   792	    chunks = []
   793	    for page_data in pages:
   794	        words = page_data["text"].split()
   795	        start = 0
   796	        while start < len(words):
   797	            end = start + CHUNK_SIZE
   798	            chunk_words = words[start:end]
   799	            chunk_text  = " ".join(chunk_words)
   800
```

## Matches in edgeAI/Claude-Nvidia Jetson project ideas for internship.txt

### L60-66
```
Unified memory is the big deal. Unlike a desktop where CPU has its own RAM and GPU has VRAM, Jetson uses a single memory pool shared between both. This means a 8GB Orin Nano actually gives your LLM the full 8GB — no separate VRAM limit. That's why you can run a 7B model at 4-bit quantization on it at all.

JetPack is your OS. When they hand you a Jetson, it'll be running JetPack — which is Ubuntu 22.04 with NVIDIA's CUDA, cuDNN, TensorRT, and a bunch of other libraries pre-installed. Don't install a vanilla Ubuntu on it or you'll lose all the GPU tooling. Think of it as their equivalent of a driver bundle.

jetson-containers is your best friend. It's an NVIDIA-maintained GitHub repo of pre-built Docker containers specifically for Jetson — Ollama, LlamaIndex, ChromaDB, Whisper, all pre-compiled for the ARM64 + CUDA combo. This saves you hours because you can't just pip install most ML libraries on Jetson's ARM architecture — they need to be compiled against Jetson's CUDA libraries specifically.

TOPS is marketing, memory is the real constraint. For your RAG project, what limits you isn't raw compute — it's how big a model you can fit in memory. On 8GB, you're looking at 7B parameter models at 4-bit quantization (roughly 4–5GB), leaving 3GB for your embedding model + ChromaDB + the app itself. Tight but doable.
```

### L302-306
```
OCR quality will bottleneck everything upstream. If PaddleOCR misreads a student's handwriting, the RAG query is garbage, the retrieval is wrong, and the evaluation is wrong — and the 1B model won't be smart enough to recover from noisy input the way a 7B would. Mitigation: scope v1 to printed/typed answer sheets only, not handwritten. Be explicit about this constraint in your problem statement.

Jetson deployment is a separate non-trivial task. People underestimate this. Getting it running on your laptop is one thing — cross-compiling, setting up JetPack, managing ARM64 Docker containers, and making sure Ollama actually uses the GPU on Jetson is easily a week of work on its own. Mitigation: allocate dedicated time in week 5-6 specifically for this, don't leave it for the end.

"Exposing as web interface" needs to be minimal. Don't build a fancy frontend — a FastAPI backend with a simple HTML file upload form is sufficient and takes 2 days not 2 weeks.
```

### L3441-3453
```
3. CPU or GPU — and do you need CUDA code?

Right now on your laptop — CPU only. Ollama uses your RTX 2050 for the LLM inference automatically (it detects CUDA on startup), but the embedding model (all-MiniLM) and reranker run on CPU via PyTorch. That's fine for dev.

On Jetson — Ollama automatically uses the Jetson GPU via CUDA once JetPack is set up. You don't write a single line of CUDA code. The stack handles it:

Ollama → uses CUDA for LLM inference automatically

FAISS → we installed faiss-cpu, which is fine. The index search is fast enough on CPU even on Jetson since the index is small

Embedding + reranker → run on CPU via PyTorch, acceptable latency for a 1B pipeline

The only thing you'd need explicit CUDA code for is if you wanted to run the embedding model on GPU too — but that's unnecessary optimization for this project scope. The bottleneck is always LLM generation, not embedding, and Ollama handles that.
```

### L6393-6409
```
Why this happens — the core issue

Jetson boards don't use regular x86 CPUs — they use ARM64 (aarch64) processors with NVIDIA's own GPU architecture (CUDA on Tegra). Most Python packages on PyPI — numpy, scipy, torch, faiss-cpu — publish prebuilt binary wheels for x86_64 Linux, macOS, Windows. They often do not publish prebuilt wheels for aarch64, especially versions compiled to work with Jetson's specific CUDA/cuDNN setup.

When you run pip install numpy==2.2.6 on Jetson with no matching wheel, pip falls back to building from source — which sometimes succeeds but creates a version that's incompatible with other system libraries that were specifically compiled by NVIDIA for that exact JetPack release.

Why the system already has its own versions

JetPack — the OS image NVIDIA ships on these boards — comes with pre-compiled, pre-tested versions of numpy (1.21.5), pandas (1.3.5), scipy (1.8.0) that are specifically built and linked against Jetson's CUDA libraries, cuDNN, and TensorRT. These aren't random old versions — they're the versions NVIDIA validated to work correctly with the board's GPU drivers.

When you pip install a different version on top, pip doesn't know about this delicate system-level compatibility. It just sees "I need numpy 2.2.6" and installs it into your user/venv site-packages, which then shadows the system one. Now your scipy (which was compiled against numpy 1.21.5's C API) tries to import the new numpy 2.2.6 and the ABI (binary interface) doesn't match — that's your dependency conflict.

Why this didn't happen on your laptop

Your laptop is x86_64 with generic numpy/scipy wheels that don't care about underlying GPU driver versions in the same tightly-coupled way. Jetson's whole software stack — JetPack, CUDA, cuDNN, TensorRT, and the Python ML packages — are version-locked together by NVIDIA. It's a much more fragile, tightly-coupled environment than a normal Linux machine.

Why Ollama wasn't installed
```

### L6413-6419
```
The fundamental lesson

On Jetson, you generally should not pip install your way to whatever version you want the way you do on a normal machine. The correct approach is: use the versions JetPack ships with wherever possible, and only pip install packages that don't have a system-provided version, ideally using NVIDIA's own prebuilt wheels for ARM64 + CUDA (they publish these for things like PyTorch specifically for Jetson) rather than generic PyPI wheels.

That's the why. Ready for me to walk through the actual fix now, tailored to what your project specifically needs (not the generic script that removes things like torch and pandas you may not even use)?

User:
```

```
Step 2 — check what numpy version is already on the system
```

## Root causes, now confirmed (not guessed)

1. **This is a shared, ephemeral, containerized "Jetson"** (AiProff Edge AI Cloud Lab), not a bare-metal board with a fixed IP. Every session in the log has a **different container hostname** (`988cf0530c3f`, `441b38050c99`, `df89f984559e`, `ea62f88c33ae`, `bb7236c5125a`). This confirms: **nothing survives between separately-booked sessions** except what you push to GitHub. Ollama, models, venvs, apt packages — all gone on next login. Budget 5-10 minutes every single session just for `zstd` + Ollama install + model pull, no way around it on this platform.

2. **No real SSH/SCP was ever going to work.** `hostname -I` returns only Docker-internal IPs (`172.17.x.x`/`172.18.x.x`). Combined with your Jio AirFibre connection using CGNAT (no public inbound IP), the mentor's "SSH in and scp" instructions were simply inapplicable to this platform. GitHub clone was correctly identified as the only real transfer path — this validates the git-based transfer approach in my last script.

3. **The `.local` vs `.venv` shadowing war was the single biggest time-sink.** Stray `pip install --user` commands (from early troubleshooting) put a second, conflicting copy of `numpy`/`sentence-transformers`/`transformers`/`scikit-learn` into `~/.local/lib/python3.10/site-packages`. Because Python's site-packages resolution order let `.local` win in some execution contexts (interactive `-c` shells behaved differently than `python3 -m uvicorn` in some runs), the exact same import worked in one shell and crashed in another — "it works in `python3 -c` but crashes under uvicorn" was **the same bug appearing twice**, not two bugs.

4. **This confirms `--system-site-packages` is correct and necessary here** — `torch==2.5.0a0+872d972e41.nv24.08` visible in the log is NVIDIA's own Jetson-specific CUDA build of torch, pre-installed at the system level. Without `--system-site-packages`, you'd lose GPU-accelerated torch entirely and fall back to a generic CPU wheel. My earlier caveat about this flag being "unverified" is resolved: keep it.

5. **The FAISS "input not a numpy array" error was a symptom of #3, not a real cross-platform FAISS incompatibility.** The embedding model's `.encode()` output looked fine in isolation but broke under the polluted import chain. Rebuilding the FAISS index natively on the board was a reasonable defensive fix regardless, but the root fix was cleaning `.local` pollution first.

6. **Disk space is a real, recurring hard constraint on this platform** — the container ships with ~108-111GB already consumed by the base image, leaving only ~3-5GB of headroom for your venv + HF model downloads + Ollama blobs. "No space left on device" killed a session outright. This needs to be actively managed every session, not discovered reactively.

7. **The exact deploy script you pasted to me last time is a distillation of this log's final message** — which explains precisely why it had the `sentence-transformers2.7.0` (missing `==`) typo and the `with open(...):` indentation bug: they were introduced when the log's final answer got hand-condensed into a script. Good news: this confirms `sentence-transformers==2.7.0` + `transformers==4.39.3` is the actual battle-tested working combo (matches what I already pinned in `backend/requirements.txt`), not a guess.

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run from the project root (edgeAI/). Run preflight_check.sh first.
#
# CONFIRMED from a real deployment log on this exact platform (AiProff Edge AI
# Cloud Lab): each booked session spins up a FRESH container (different
# hostname every time — e.g. 988cf0530c3f, 441b38050c99, df89f984559e...).
# Nothing survives between separately-booked sessions except what's in git.
# Budget ~5-10 minutes of every session for zstd + Ollama install + model pull
# — there is no way to skip this by caching across sessions on this platform.
set -e

# PYTHONNOUSERSITE prevents Python from also loading ~/.local/lib/pythonX/site-packages.
# Root cause of the single biggest time-sink in the real deployment log: stray
# `pip install --user` runs (from earlier troubleshooting) left a second,
# conflicting numpy/sentence-transformers/transformers in ~/.local, which
# shadowed the clean venv copies in some execution contexts (uvicorn subprocess)
# but not others (interactive `python3 -c`) — same bug, looked like two bugs.
export PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1

# Cache dirs are only useful for reruns *within* the same session — see the
# ephemeral-container note above. Still worth setting so a retry after a
# crash doesn't re-download inside the same booking.
export HF_HOME="${HF_HOME:-$PWD/.cache/huggingface}"
export OLLAMA_MODELS="${OLLAMA_MODELS:-$PWD/.cache/ollama}"
mkdir -p "$HF_HOME" "$OLLAMA_MODELS"

wait_for() {
    # wait_for <url> <label> <max_seconds>
    local url="$1" label="$2" max="$3" waited=0
    until curl -sf "$url" -o /dev/null 2>/dev/null; do
        waited=$((waited + 2))
        if [ "$waited" -ge "$max" ]; then
            echo "❌ Timed out waiting for $label ($url) after ${max}s"
            return 1
        fi
        sleep 2
    done
    echo "✅ $label is up after ${waited}s"
}

echo "== Step 0: Sanity checks =="
[ -f backend/.env ] || { echo "❌ backend/.env missing — copy it before continuing."; exit 1; }
[ -f data/index/chunks.pkl ] || { echo "❌ data/index/chunks.pkl missing — copy your prebuilt index files (they're gitignored) before continuing."; exit 1; }
PYVER=$(python3 -c 'import sys; print(sys.version_info[:2]>=(3,10))')
[ "$PYVER" = "True" ] || { echo "❌ python3 is older than 3.10 — faiss-cpu==1.14.2 will not install."; exit 1; }

echo "== Step 0.5: Disk hygiene =="
# Real log evidence: this platform's containers ship with ~108-111GB already
# consumed by the base image, leaving only ~3-5GB headroom. "No space left on
# device" killed a whole session in the past. Clean proactively, don't wait
# for the error.
pip cache purge >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
sudo apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
# Known stray-package cleanup — remove any leftover ~/.local copies of packages
# this project uses, so PYTHONNOUSERSITE isn't the only thing preventing them
# from causing confusion later (e.g. if some other tool re-enables user site).
for pkg in numpy scipy sentence_transformers transformers sklearn faiss; do
    rm -rf "$HOME"/.local/lib/python3.*/site-packages/"$pkg"* 2>/dev/null || true
done
AVAIL_KB=$(df --output=avail -k / | tail -1)
echo "Free space on /: $((AVAIL_KB / 1024)) MB"
if [ "$AVAIL_KB" -lt 1500000 ]; then
    echo "⚠️  Less than ~1.5GB free on / — this has caused hard failures before."
    echo "    Consider: sudo apt-get autoremove -y; rm -rf ~/.cache/* (except HF_HOME/OLLAMA_MODELS above)"
fi

echo "== Step 1: Install zstd =="
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y zstd

echo "== Step 2: Install Ollama =="
curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh

echo "== Step 3: Start Ollama =="
ollama serve > /tmp/ollama.log 2>&1 &
wait_for "http://localhost:11434" "Ollama server" 30

echo "== Step 4: Pull model =="
ollama pull llama3.2:1b

echo "== Step 5: Setup Python =="
# --system-site-packages is deliberate and confirmed necessary: JetPack ships
# an NVIDIA-built torch (seen in the real log as torch==2.5.0a0+...nv24.08)
# with CUDA/Tegra support. A plain venv would lose that and fall back to a
# generic CPU-only PyPI wheel.
python3 -m venv .venv --system-site-packages
source .venv/bin/activate

echo "== Step 6: Install packages (single source of truth: requirements.txt) =="
pip install --no-user -r backend/requirements.txt

echo "== Step 6.5: Verify no .local contamination =="
python3 -c "
import sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers
for mod in (sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers):
    path = mod.__file__
    assert '.local' not in path, f'{mod.__name__} is loading from .local: {path}'
    print(mod.__name__, '->', path)
print('clean: all imports resolve inside .venv')
"

echo "== Step 7: Rebuild FAISS index natively (avoids any residual numpy/FAISS ABI mismatch) =="
cd backend
python3 - <<'PYEOF'
import pickle
import faiss
import numpy as np
from ingest import embed_model

INDEX_DIR = "../data/index"

with open(f"{INDEX_DIR}/chunks.pkl", "rb") as f:
    chunks = pickle.load(f)

texts = [c["text"] for c in chunks]
embeddings = embed_model.encode(
    texts, convert_to_numpy=True, normalize_embeddings=True
)
embeddings = np.ascontiguousarray(embeddings, dtype=np.float32)

index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(embeddings.shape[1])
index.add(embeddings)
faiss.write_index(index, f"{INDEX_DIR}/faiss.index")
print(f"Done: {index.ntotal} vectors")
PYEOF

echo "== Step 8: Start backend =="
python3 -m uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 > /tmp/uvicorn.log 2>&1 &
if wait_for "http://localhost:8000/health" "Backend" 90; then
    curl http://localhost:8000/health
else
    echo "---- last 50 lines of uvicorn log ----"
    tail -n 50 /tmp/uvicorn.log
    exit 1
fi

```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run this FIRST, before deploy_jetson.sh, to fail fast (seconds, not hours)
# if something fundamental is wrong. Run from the project root (edgeAI/).
set -u
FAIL=0

check() {
    local desc="$1"; local cmd="$2"
    printf "%-55s" "$desc"
    if eval "$cmd" >/tmp/preflight_out 2>&1; then
        echo "OK"
    else
        echo "FAIL"
        sed 's/^/    /' /tmp/preflight_out
        FAIL=1
    fi
}

echo "== Architecture / Python =="
check "Architecture is aarch64"          "[ \"\$(uname -m)\" = aarch64 ]"
check "python3 is >= 3.10 (faiss-cpu needs it)" \
    "python3 -c 'import sys; assert sys.version_info >= (3,10), sys.version'"

echo
echo "== Network reachability =="
check "Can reach ollama.com"             "curl -fsS -m 8 -o /dev/null https://ollama.com"
check "Can reach huggingface.co"         "curl -fsS -m 8 -o /dev/null https://huggingface.co"
check "Can reach pypi.org"               "curl -fsS -m 8 -o /dev/null https://pypi.org"

echo
echo "== Required project files present =="
check "backend/.env exists"              "[ -f backend/.env ]"
check "backend/requirements.txt exists"  "[ -f backend/requirements.txt ]"
check "data/index/chunks.pkl exists"     "[ -f data/index/chunks.pkl ]"
check "data/index/bm25.pkl exists"       "[ -f data/index/bm25.pkl ]"
check "data/index/faiss.index exists"    "[ -f data/index/faiss.index ]"

echo
echo "== Disk / memory headroom =="
# Real observed baseline on this platform: base container image alone can
# consume ~108-111GB of a 116GB disk, leaving only ~3-5GB headroom. Checking
# '/' (not '.') because home-dir mounts have shown misleading 0-avail readings
# while '/' still had room.
check "At least 1.5GB free on /"          "[ \$(df --output=avail -k / | tail -1) -gt 1500000 ]"

echo
echo "== Reminder =="
echo "This platform gives a FRESH container every booked session (hostname"
echo "changes each time). Nothing persists across sessions except git. Budget"
echo "5-10 min every session for zstd + Ollama install + model pull."
```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run this FIRST, before deploy_jetson.sh, to fail fast (seconds, not hours)
# if something fundamental is wrong. Run from the project root (edgeAI/).
set -u
FAIL=0

check() {
    local desc="$1"; local cmd="$2"
    printf "%-55s" "$desc"
    if eval "$cmd" >/tmp/preflight_out 2>&1; then
        echo "OK"
    else
        echo "FAIL"
        sed 's/^/    /' /tmp/preflight_out
        FAIL=1
    fi
}

echo "== Architecture / Python =="
check "Architecture is aarch64"          "[ \"\$(uname -m)\" = aarch64 ]"

    "python3 -c 'import sys; assert sys.version_info >= (3,10), sys.version'"

echo "== Network reachability =="

check "Can reach huggingface.co"         "curl -fsS -m 8 -o /dev/null https://huggingface.co"
check "Can reach pypi.org"               "curl -fsS -m 8 -o /dev/null https://pypi.org"

check "backend/requirements.txt exists"  "[ -f backend/requirements.txt ]"
check "data/index/chunks.pkl exists"     "[ -f data/index/chunks.pkl ]"
check "data/index/bm25.pkl exists"       "[ -f data/index/bm25.pkl ]"
check "data/index/faiss.index exists"    "[ -f data/index/faiss.index ]"

echo
echo "== Disk / memory headroom =="
# Real observed baseline on this platform: base container image alone can
# consume ~108-111GB of a 116GB disk, leaving only ~3-5GB headroom. Checking
# '/' (not '.') because home-dir mounts have shown misleading 0-avail readings
# while '/' still had room. Storage appears to persist across session
# bookings (only the container hostname resets) — a fresh-looking session
# with a full disk usually means leftover cruft from earlier attempts.
AVAIL_KB=$(df --output=avail -k / | tail -1)
echo "Free on /: $((AVAIL_KB / 1024)) MB"
check "At least 1.5GB free on /"          "[ \$AVAIL_KB -gt 1500000 ]"
if [ "$AVAIL_KB" -le 1500000 ]; then
    echo "  Biggest items in \$HOME:"
    du -h --max-depth=1 "$HOME" 2>/dev/null | sort -rh | head -8 | sed 's/^/    /'
    echo "  Try: pip cache purge; rm -rf ~/.cache/pip; sudo apt-get clean"
    echo "  Also check: du -sh ~/.ollama ~/.cache/

echo
echo "== Reminder =="
echo "This platform gives a FRESH container every booked session (hostname"
echo "changes each time). Nothing persists across sessions except git. Budget"
echo "5-10 min every session for zstd + Ollama install + model pull."

echo

    echo "❌ Preflight FAILED — fix the items above before running deploy_jetson.sh"
```

## Diagnosis

- `~/.local` = **3.9GB** — this is almost your entire disk problem, and it's literally the exact ".local vs .venv" package-shadowing poison from the historical debugging saga (leftover `pip install --user` installs of numpy/torch/sentence-transformers from earlier sessions).
```
#!/bin/bash
# Run from the project root (edgeAI/). Run preflight_check.sh first.
#
# CONFIRMED from a real deployment log on this exact platform (AiProff Edge AI
# Cloud Lab): each booked session spins up a FRESH container (different
# hostname every time — e.g. 988cf0530c3f, 441b38050c99, df89f984559e...).
# Nothing survives between separately-booked sessions except what's in git.
# Budget ~5-10 minutes of every session for zstd + Ollama install + model pull
# — there is no way to skip this by caching across sessions on this platform.
set -e

# PYTHONNOUSERSITE prevents Python from also loading ~/.local/lib/pythonX/site-packages.
# Root cause of the single biggest time-sink in the real deployment log: stray
# `pip install --user` runs (from earlier troubleshooting) left a second,
# conflicting numpy/sentence-transformers/transformers in ~/.local, which
# shadowed the clean venv copies in some execution contexts (uvicorn subprocess)
# but not others (interactive `python3 -c`) — same bug, looked like two bugs.
export PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1

# Cache dirs are only useful for reruns *within* the same session — see the
# ephemeral-container note above. Still worth setting so a retry after a
# crash doesn't re-download inside the same booking.
export HF_HOME="${HF_HOME:-$PWD/.cache/huggingface}"
export OLLAMA_MODELS="${OLLAMA_MODELS:-$PWD/.cache/ollama}"
mkdir -p "$HF_HOME" "$OLLAMA_MODELS"

wait_for() {
    # wait_for <url> <label> <max_seconds>
    local url="$1" label="$2" max="$3" waited=0
    until curl -sf "$url" -o /dev/null 2>/dev/null; do
        waited=$((waited + 2))
        if [ "$waited" -ge "$max" ]; then
            echo "❌ Timed out waiting for $label ($url) after ${max}s"
            return 1
        fi
        sleep 2
    done
    echo "✅ $label is up after ${waited}s"
}

echo "== Step 0: Sanity checks =="
[ -f backend/.env ] || { echo "❌ backend/.env missing — copy it before continuing."; exit 1; }
[ -f data/index/chunks.pkl ] || { echo "❌ data/index/chunks.pkl missing — copy your prebuilt index files (they're gitignored) before continuing."; exit 1; }
PYVER=$(python3 -c 'import sys; print(sys.version_info[:2]>=(3,10))')
[ "$PYVER" = "True" ] || { echo "❌ python3 is older than 3.10 — faiss-cpu==1.14.2 will not install."; exit 1; }

echo "== Step 0.5: Disk hygiene =="
# Real log evidence: this platform's containers ship with ~108-111GB already
# consumed by the base image, leaving only ~3-5GB headroom. "No space left on
# device" killed a whole session in the past. Clean proactively, don't wait
# for the error.
pip cache purge >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
rm -rf "$HOME/.cache/pip" 2>/dev/null || true
sudo apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
# Confirmed on real hardware: ~/.local accumulates to multiple GB (torch,
# numpy, sentence-transformers, transformers, sklearn from old `pip install
# --user` runs across earlier sessions) and has zero legitimate use in this
# workflow — it's the exact source of the .local-vs-.venv import shadowing
# bug documented in the deployment log. Wholesale removal, not partial.
if [ -d "$HOME/.local" ]; then
    FREED=$(du -sh "$HOME/.local" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1)
    echo "Removing ~/.local ($FREED) — leftover user-site packages from earlier sessions"
    rm -rf "$HOME/.local"
fi
# NOTE: intentionally NOT touching ~/.ollama — pulled models cache there and
# storage has been observed to persist across session bookings, so it may
# already contain llama3.2:1b from a previous session.
AVAIL_KB=$(df --output=avail -k / | tail -1)
echo "Free space on /: $((AVAIL_KB / 1024)) MB"
if [ "$AVAIL_KB" -lt 1500000 ]; then
    echo "⚠️  Less than ~1.5GB free on / — this has caused hard failures before."
    echo "    Consider: sudo apt-get autoremove -y; rm -rf ~/.cache/* (except HF_HOME/OLLAMA_MODELS above)"
fi

echo "== Step 1: Install zstd =="
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y zstd

echo "== Step 2: Install Ollama =="
curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh

echo "== Step 3: Start Ollama =="
ollama serve > /tmp/ollama.log 2>&1 &
wait_for "http://localhost:11434" "Ollama server" 30

echo "== Step 4: Pull model =="
ollama pull llama3.2:1b

echo "== Step 5: Setup Python =="
# --system-site-packages is deliberate and confirmed necessary: JetPack ships
# an NVIDIA-built torch (seen in the real log as torch==2.5.0a0+...nv24.08)
# with CUDA/Tegra support. A plain venv would lose that and fall back to a
# generic CPU-only PyPI wheel.
python3 -m venv .venv --system-site-packages
source .venv/bin/activate

echo "== Step 6: Install packages (single source of truth: requirements.txt) =="
pip install --no-user -r backend/requirements.txt

echo "== Step 6.5: Verify no .local contamination =="
python3 -c "
import sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers
for mod in (sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers):
    path = mod.__file__
    assert '.local' not in path, f'{mod.__name__} is loading from .local: {path}'
    print(mod.__name__, '->', path)
print('clean: all imports resolve inside .venv')
"

echo "== Step 7: Rebuild FAISS index natively (avoids any residual numpy/FAISS ABI mismatch) =="
cd backend
python3 - <<'PYEOF'
import pickle
import faiss
import numpy as np
from ingest import embed_model

INDEX_DIR = "../data/index"

with open(f"{INDEX_DIR}/chunks.pkl", "rb") as f:
    chunks = pickle.load(f)

texts = [c["text"] for c in chunks]
embeddings = embed_model.encode(
    texts, convert_to_numpy=True, normalize_embeddings=True
)
embeddings = np.ascontiguousarray(embeddings, dtype=np.float32)

index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(embeddings.shape[1])
index.add(embeddings)
faiss.write_index(index, f"{INDEX_DIR}/faiss.index")
print(f"Done: {index.ntotal} vectors")
PYEOF

echo "== Step 8: Start backend =="
python3 -m uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 > /tmp/uvicorn.log 2>&1 &
if wait_for "http://localhost:8000/health" "Backend" 90; then
    curl http://localhost:8000/health
else
    echo "---- last 50 lines of uvicorn log ----"
    tail -n 50 /tmp/uvicorn.log
    exit 1
fi

```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run from the project root (edgeAI/). Run preflight_check.sh first.
#
# CONFIRMED from a real deployment log on this exact platform (AiProff Edge AI
# Cloud Lab): each booked session spins up a FRESH container (different
# hostname every time — e.g. 988cf0530c3f, 441b38050c99, df89f984559e...).
# Nothing survives between separately-booked sessions except what's in git.
# Budget ~5-10 minutes of every session for zstd + Ollama install + model pull
# — there is no way to skip this by caching across sessions on this platform.
set -e

# PYTHONNOUSERSITE prevents Python from also loading ~/.local/lib/pythonX/site-packages.
# Root cause of the single biggest time-sink in the real deployment log: stray
# `pip install --user` runs (from earlier troubleshooting) left a second,
# conflicting numpy/sentence-transformers/transformers in ~/.local, which
# shadowed the clean venv copies in some execution contexts (uvicorn subprocess)
# but not others (interactive `python3 -c`) — same bug, looked like two bugs.
export PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1

# Cache dirs are only useful for reruns *within* the same session — see the
# ephemeral-container note above. Still worth setting so a retry after a
# crash doesn't re-download inside the same booking.
export HF_HOME="${HF_HOME:-$PWD/.cache/huggingface}"
export OLLAMA_MODELS="${OLLAMA_MODELS:-$PWD/.cache/ollama}"
mkdir -p "$HF_HOME" "$OLLAMA_MODELS"

wait_for() {
    # wait_for <url> <label> <max_seconds>
    local url="$1" label="$2" max="$3" waited=0
    until curl -sf "$url" -o /dev/null 2>/dev/null; do
        waited=$((waited + 2))
        if [ "$waited" -ge "$max" ]; then
            echo "❌ Timed out waiting for $label ($url) after ${max}s"
            return 1
        fi
        sleep 2
    done
    echo "✅ $label is up after ${waited}s"
}

echo "== Step 0: Sanity checks =="
[ -f backend/.env ] || { echo "❌ backend/.env missing — copy it before continuing."; exit 1; }
[ -f data/index/chunks.pkl ] || { echo "❌ data/index/chunks.pkl missing — copy your prebuilt index files (they're gitignored) before continuing."; exit 1; }
PYVER=$(python3 -c 'import sys; print(sys.version_info[:2]>=(3,10))')
[ "$PYVER" = "True" ] || { echo "❌ python3 is older than 3.10 — faiss-cpu==1.14.2 will not install."; exit 1; }

echo "== Step 0.5: Disk hygiene =="
# Real log evidence: this platform's containers ship with ~108-111GB already
# consumed by the base image, leaving only ~3-5GB headroom. "No space left on
# device" killed a whole session in the past. Clean proactively, don't wait
# for the error.
pip cache purge >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
rm -rf "$HOME/.cache/pip" 2>/dev/null || true
sudo apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
# Confirmed on real hardware: ~/.local accumulates to multiple GB (torch,
# numpy, sentence-transformers, transformers, sklearn from old `pip install
# --user` runs across earlier sessions) and has zero legitimate use in this
# workflow — it's the exact source of the .local-vs-.venv import shadowing
# bug documented in the deployment log. Wholesale removal, not partial.
if [ -d "$HOME/.local" ]; then
    FREED=$(du -sh "$HOME/.local" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1)
    echo "Removing ~/.local ($FREED) — leftover user-site packages from earlier sessions"
    rm -rf "$HOME/.local"
fi
# NOTE: intentionally NOT touching ~/.ollama — pulled models cache there and
# storage has been observed to persist across session bookings, so it may
# already contain llama3.2:1b from a previous session.
AVAIL_KB=$(df --output=avail -k / | tail -1)
echo "Free space on /: $((AVAIL_KB / 1024)) MB"
if [ "$AVAIL_KB" -lt 1500000 ]; then
    echo "⚠️  Less than ~1.5GB free on / — this has caused hard failures before."
    echo "    Consider: sudo apt-get autoremove -y; rm -rf ~/.cache/* (except HF_HOME/OLLAMA_MODELS above)"
fi

echo "== Step 1: Install zstd =="
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y zstd

echo "== Step 2: Install Ollama =="
curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh

echo "== Step 3: Start Ollama =="
ollama serve > /tmp/ollama.log 2>&1 &
wait_for "http://localhost:11434" "Ollama server" 30

echo "== Step 4: Pull model =="
if ollama list 2>/dev/null | grep -q 'llama3.2:1b'; then
    echo "llama3.2:1b already cached — skipping download"
else
    ollama pull llama3.2:1b
fi

echo "== Step 5: Setup Python =="
# --system-site-packages is deliberate and confirmed necessary: JetPack ships
# an NVIDIA-built torch (seen in the real log as torch==2.5.0a0+...nv24.08)
# with CUDA/Tegra support. A plain venv would lose that and fall back to a
# generic CPU-only PyPI wheel.
python3 -m venv .venv --system-site-packages
source .venv/bin/activate

echo "== Step 6: Install packages (single source of truth: requirements.txt) =="
pip install --no-user -r backend/requirements.txt

echo "== Step 6.5: Verify no .local contamination =="
python3 -c "
import sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers
for mod in (sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers):
    path = mod.__file__
    assert '.local' not in path, f'{mod.__name__} is loading from .local: {path}'
    print(mod.__name__, '->', path)
print('clean: all imports resolve inside .venv')
"

echo "== Step 7: Rebuild FAISS index natively (avoids any residual numpy/FAISS ABI mismatch) =="
cd backend
python3 - <<'PYEOF'
import pickle
import faiss
import numpy as np
from ingest import embed_model

INDEX_DIR = "../data/index"

with open(f"{INDEX_DIR}/chunks.pkl", "rb") as f:
    chunks = pickle.load(f)

texts = [c["text"] for c in chunks]
embeddings = embed_model.encode(
    texts, convert_to_numpy=True, normalize_embeddings=True
)
embeddings = np.ascontiguousarray(embeddings, dtype=np.float32)

index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(embeddings.shape[1])
index.add(embeddings)
faiss.write_index(index, f"{INDEX_DIR}/faiss.index")
print(f"Done: {index.ntotal} vectors")
PYEOF

echo "== Step 8: Start backend =="
python3 -m uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 > /tmp/uvicorn.log 2>&1 &
if wait_for "http://localhost:8000/health" "Backend" 90; then
    curl http://localhost:8000/health
else
    echo "---- last 50 lines of uvicorn log ----"
    tail -n 50 /tmp/uvicorn.log
    exit 1
fi
```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run from the project root (edgeAI/). Run preflight_check.sh first.
#
# CONFIRMED from a real deployment log on this exact platform (AiProff Edge AI
# Cloud Lab): each booked session spins up a FRESH container (different
# hostname every time — e.g. 988cf0530c3f, 441b38050c99, df89f984559e...).
# Nothing survives between separately-booked sessions except what's in git.
# Budget ~5-10 minutes of every session for zstd + Ollama install + model pull
# — there is no way to skip this by caching across sessions on this platform.
set -e

# PYTHONNOUSERSITE prevents Python from also loading ~/.local/lib/pythonX/site-packages.
# Root cause of the single biggest time-sink in the real deployment log: stray
# `pip install --user` runs (from earlier troubleshooting) left a second,
# conflicting numpy/sentence-transformers/transformers in ~/.local, which
# shadowed the clean venv copies in some execution contexts (uvicorn subprocess)
# but not others (interactive `python3 -c`) — same bug, looked like two bugs.
export PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1

# Cache dirs are only useful for reruns *within* the same session — see the
# ephemeral-container note above. Still worth setting so a retry after a
# crash doesn't re-download inside the same booking.
export HF_HOME="${HF_HOME:-$PWD/.cache/huggingface}"
export OLLAMA_MODELS="${OLLAMA_MODELS:-$PWD/.cache/ollama}"
mkdir -p "$HF_HOME" "$OLLAMA_MODELS"

wait_for() {
    # wait_for <url> <label> <max_seconds>
    local url="$1" label="$2" max="$3" waited=0
    until curl -sf "$url" -o /dev/null 2>/dev/null; do
        waited=$((waited + 2))
        if [ "$waited" -ge "$max" ]; then
            echo "❌ Timed out waiting for $label ($url) after ${max}s"
            return 1
        fi
        sleep 2
    done
    echo "✅ $label is up after ${waited}s"
}

echo "== Step 0: Sanity checks =="
[ -f backend/.env ] || { echo "❌ backend/.env missing — copy it before continuing."; exit 1; }
[ -f data/index/chunks.pkl ] || { echo "❌ data/index/chunks.pkl missing — copy your prebuilt index files (they're gitignored) before continuing."; exit 1; }
PYVER=$(python3 -c 'import sys; print(sys.version_info[:2]>=(3,10))')
[ "$PYVER" = "True" ] || { echo "❌ python3 is older than 3.10 — faiss-cpu==1.14.2 will not install."; exit 1; }

echo "== Step 0.5: Disk hygiene =="
# Real log evidence: this platform's containers ship with ~108-111GB already
# consumed by the base image, leaving only ~3-5GB headroom. "No space left on
# device" killed a whole session in the past. Clean proactively, don't wait
# for the error.
pip cache purge >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
rm -rf "$HOME/.cache/pip" 2>/dev/null || true
sudo apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || apt-get clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
# Confirmed on real hardware: ~/.local accumulates to multiple GB (torch,
# numpy, sentence-transformers, transformers, sklearn from old `pip install
# --user` runs across earlier sessions) and has zero legitimate use in this
# workflow — it's the exact source of the .local-vs-.venv import shadowing
# bug documented in the deployment log. Wholesale removal, not partial.
if [ -d "$HOME/.local" ]; then
    FREED=$(du -sh "$HOME/.local" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1)
    echo "Removing ~/.local ($FREED) — leftover user-site packages from earlier sessions"
    rm -rf "$HOME/.local"
fi
# NOTE: intentionally NOT touching ~/.ollama — pulled models cache there and
# storage has been observed to persist across session bookings, so it may
# already contain llama3.2:1b from a previous session.
AVAIL_KB=$(df --output=avail -k / | tail -1)
echo "Free space on /: $((AVAIL_KB / 1024)) MB"
if [ "$AVAIL_KB" -lt 1500000 ]; then
    echo "⚠️  Less than ~1.5GB free on / — this has caused hard failures before."
    echo "    Consider: sudo apt-get autoremove -y; rm -rf ~/.cache/* (except HF_HOME/OLLAMA_MODELS above)"
fi

echo "== Step 1: Install zstd =="
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y zstd

echo "== Step 2: Install Ollama =="
curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh

echo "== Step 3: Start Ollama =="
ollama serve > /tmp/ollama.log 2>&1 &
wait_for "http://localhost:11434" "Ollama server" 30

echo "== Step 4: Pull model =="
if ollama list 2>/dev/null | grep -q 'llama3.2:1b'; then
    echo "llama3.2:1b already cached — skipping download"
else
    ollama pull llama3.2:1b
fi

echo "== Step 5: Setup Python =="
# --system-site-packages is deliberate and confirmed necessary: JetPack ships
# an NVIDIA-built torch (seen in the real log as torch==2.5.0a0+...nv24.08)
# with CUDA/Tegra support. A plain venv would lose that and fall back to a
# generic CPU-only PyPI wheel.
# Real failure mode hit on this platform: "ensurepip is not available" because
# python3.10-venv isn't installed and the base image is partially read-only.
# Try the normal path first, fall back to a pip-less venv + manual bootstrap
# rather than aborting the whole run.
rm -rf .venv
if ! python3 -m venv .venv --system-site-packages 2>/tmp/venv_err; then
    echo "Normal venv creation failed:"; cat /tmp/venv_err
    echo "Trying: apt install python3.10-venv ..."
    (sudo apt-get install -y python3.10-venv || apt-get install -y python3.10-venv) 2>/dev/null || true
    rm -rf .venv
    if ! python3 -m venv .venv --system-site-packages 2>/tmp/venv_err2; then
        echo "Still failing, falling back to --without-pip + get-pip.py bootstrap"
        cat /tmp/venv_err2
        rm -rf .venv
        python3 -m venv .venv --system-site-packages --without-pip
        source .venv/bin/activate
        curl -sS https://bootstrap.pypa.io/get-pip.py -o /tmp/get-pip.py
        python3 /tmp/get-pip.py --no-warn-script-location
    else
        source .venv/bin/activate
    fi
else
    source .venv/bin/activate
fi

echo "== Step 6: Install packages (single source of truth: requirements.txt) =="
pip install --no-user -r backend/requirements.txt

echo "== Step 6.5: Verify no .local contamination =="
python3 -c "
import sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers
for mod in (sentence_transformers, numpy, transformers):
    path = mod.__file__
    assert '.local' not in path, f'{mod.__name__} is loading from .local: {path}'
    print(mod.__name__, '->', path)
print('clean: all imports resolve inside .venv')
"

echo "== Step 7: Rebuild FAISS index natively (avoids any residual numpy/FAISS ABI mismatch) =="
cd backend
python3 - <<'PYEOF'
import pickle
import faiss
import numpy as np
from ingest import embed_model

INDEX_DIR = "../data/index"

with open(f"{INDEX_DIR}/chunks.pkl", "rb") as f:
    chunks = pickle.load(f)

texts = [c["text"] for c in chunks]
embeddings = embed_model.encode(
    texts, convert_to_numpy=True, normalize_embeddings=True
)
embeddings = np.ascontiguousarray(embeddings, dtype=np.float32)

index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(embeddings.shape[1])
index.add(embeddings)
faiss.write_index(index, f"{INDEX_DIR}/faiss.index")
print(f"Done: {index.ntotal} vectors")
PYEOF

echo "== Step 8: Start backend =="
python3 -m uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 > /tmp/uvicorn.log 2>&1 &
if wait_for "http://localhost:8000/health" "Backend" 90; then
    curl http://localhost:8000/health
else
    echo "---- last 50 lines of uvicorn log ----"
    tail -n 50 /tmp/uvicorn.log
    exit 1
fi

```

## Assistant

<thinking>
I'm noticing a version mismatch issue with sentence_transformers—the installed version appears to be older than the pinned 2.7.0, and the import statements in the __init__.py file don't align with what I'd expect from that version. I'm reconsidering whether the datasets import is actually a sign of an old version or if it's a compatibility layer that sentence-transformers 2.7.0 maintains for backward compatibility with legacy training code. The SentencesDataset might still be present in the __init__.py for older-style .fit() API support even in recent versions, so this traceback alone doesn't necessarily prove the wrong version was installed.

The actual root cause is deeper: sklearn's utilities are pulling in the system-level pandas from /usr/lib/python3/dist-packages, which then tries to import pytz, and pytz fails because it can't find the tzdata.zi file at /usr/share/zoneinfo/tzdata.zi on this container. This missing timezone data file is what's cascading the import failure, completely separate from any numpy ABI or venv contamination issues.

The fix would be to reinstall the tzdata package, though that might run into the same permission issues we've seen with apt locks. They should retry with clean commands: running `sudo apt-get update` followed by `sudo apt-get install --reinstall -y tzdata` to fix the timezone data issue. Alternatively, I could patch pytz's version-reading function to avoid the crash entirely, or sidestep the whole import chain by addressing why scikit-learn is pulling in pandas just for version parsing — though that dependency is baked into this version of sklearn and would be difficult to work around without modifying the library itself.

The fastest approach that avoids needing root or apt is to manually create the missing tzdata.zi file or set an environment variable to bypass pytz's check. The issue stems from pytz trying to read `/usr/share/zoneinfo/tzdata.zi` for version information, which is often missing in minimal container images since it's part of the tzdata package's zoneinfo data. I'm checking whether pytz has a fallback mechanism when that file doesn't exist, or if there's an environment override that would let it skip this validation step entirely. I'm second-guessing myself on how pytz actually works — the system-installed `python3-pytz` package from apt might be the culprit here, since it's trying to read system zoneinfo files that don't exist, whereas a fresh pip install of pytz would bundle its own zoneinfo data and avoid this dependency entirely. So the system pandas gets picked up as a fallback since we're using `--system-site-packages` and pandas isn't explicitly installed in the venv itself—it's only a transitive dependency of scikit-learn that's used for a single utility function. Python resolves to the system-installed version, which brings along the Debian-patched pytz that expects the missing tzdata index file.

The fix is straightforward: install pandas directly into the venv with `pip install --no-user pandas`. This gives us a clean, pip-installed copy with its own bundled timezone data that takes precedence over the system version in the import path, completely bypassing the tzdata issue without needing root access. After installing, I'll re-run the verification script to confirm it works. Pandas should have prebuilt wheels available for aarch64 on Python 3.10, so installation will be fast without needing to compile from source. </thinking>

This is a *different* bug — not `.local` contamination (confirmed clean, your traceback path is all `.venv`). The real crash is `pytz` (a transitive dependency: `sklearn` → `pandas` → `pytz`) trying to read `/usr/share/zoneinfo/tzdata.zi` from the system, which this container's stripped-down image is missing. It's picking up the **system** `pandas`/`pytz` because neither is installed inside your venv, so `--system-site-packages` falls back to the broken system copies.

The issue is that even though the request completed after 30 seconds, no tokens made it through to the client — suggesting either the response stream was empty, or something broke during the iteration.

The error handling here is the culprit: if Ollama returns an error status code (like 404 for a missing model or 500 for an internal error) instead of streaming JSON, the response body lines won't parse as valid JSON. The `json.JSONDecodeError` exception gets caught and silently ignored with `continue`, so the loop just consumes the error response without yielding anything. When the connection closes without ever sending `"done": true`, the generator finishes cleanly without raising an exception — the client gets nothing, and the request appears to complete successfully from curl's perspective. The real issue is that httpx doesn't automatically raise exceptions for non-2xx status codes unless you explicitly call `raise_for_status()`, so a 500 error response with an error JSON body would still iterate through the lines and hit this silent failure mode. The fix is to add explicit error handling in the query_ollama_stream function to catch these cases.


**Then apply this fix regardless — it makes the next failure visible instead of silent.** Paste this whole block to rewrite `llm.py`:

```
cat > llm.py << 'EOF'
import os
import httpx
import json
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

OLLAMA_HOST = os.getenv("OLLAMA_HOST", "http://localhost:11434")
OLLAMA_MODEL = os.getenv("OLLAMA_MODEL", "llama3.2:1b")

def build_prompt(question: str, chunks: list[dict]) -> str:
    context_blocks = []
    for i, chunk in enumerate(chunks):
        context_blocks.append(
            f"[Source {i+1} — {chunk['source']}, page {chunk.get('page', '?')}]\n"
            f"{chunk['text']}"
        )
    context = "\n\n".join(context_blocks)

    prompt = f"""You are a precise document assistant. Answer questions using ONLY the context below.

Rules:
- Answer in one sentence or less — match the brevity of the question
- If the answer is a number, date, name, or short phrase, give ONLY that value
- If listing multiple items, separate them with commas or "and" — never use bullet points or newlines
- No greetings, no preamble, no "According to...", no "Based on..."
- Do not repeat the question
- If the answer is not in the context, respond with exactly: Not found.

Context:
{context}

Question: {question}
Answer:"""

    return prompt

async def query_ollama_stream(question: str, chunks: list[dict]):
    prompt = build_prompt(question, chunks)

    payload = {
        "model": OLLAMA_MODEL,
        "prompt": prompt,
        "stream": True,
        "options": {
            "temperature": 0.1,
            "top_p": 0.9,
            "top_k": 40,
            "repeat_penalty": 1.1,
            "num_predict": 100,
            "stop": ["\n\n", "Question:", "Context:"]
        }
    }

    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=120.0) as client:
        async with client.stream(
            "POST",
            f"{OLLAMA_HOST}/api/generate",
            json=payload
        ) as response:
            if response.status_code != 200:
                body = await response.aread()
                yield f"[Ollama HTTP {response.status_code}: {body.decode(errors='replace')[:300]}]"
                return
            got_any = False
            async for line in response.aiter_lines():
                if not line.strip():
                    continue
                try:
                    data = json.loads(line)
                except json.JSONDecodeError:
                    continue
                if data.get("error"):
                    yield f"[Ollama error: {data['error']}]"
                    return
                token = data.get("response", "")
                if token:
                    got_any = True
                    yield token
                if data.get("done"):
                    break
            if not got_any:
                yield "[No tokens received from Ollama — check /tmp/ollama.log]"

async def query_ollama(question: str, chunks: list[dict]) -> str:
    full_response = ""
    async for token in query_ollama_stream(question, chunks):
        full_response += token
    return full_response.strip()
EOF
```

**Restart the backend and retry:**
```
pkill -f uvicorn
sleep 2
python3 -m uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 > /tmp/uvicorn.log 2>&1 &
sleep 5
time curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/query -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"question": "what is academic dishonesty?"}'
```

This time you'll get actual visible text no matter what — either a real answer, or an explicit `[Ollama error: ...]`/`[Ollama HTTP ...]` message telling us exactly what's wrong. Paste whatever comes back plus the `ollama.log` tail from the first command.

Even with plenty of system RAM available, Ollama still can't allocate the GPU memory it needs, which points to a hard limit on GPU memory within the container itself—likely a cgroup or platform-level restriction that's separate from the system RAM constraints.

The most probable culprit is the Jetson's CMA (Contiguous Memory Allocator) pool, which is configured at boot time and reserves a fixed chunk of memory specifically for GPU operations. If that pool is much smaller than 1.25GB, CUDA allocations will fail regardless of how much free system RAM exists, and there's no way to fix it from within an unprivileged container without rebooting the device and reconfiguring the kernel parameters.

Since this is almost certainly a platform-level resource constraint in a cloud rental environment, I can force Ollama to run entirely on CPU by setting the GPU layer count to zero in the model options, which sidesteps the CUDA allocation issue entirely. The 1B model should still run acceptably on the Jetson's ARM cores, just slower.

The most direct approach is to hide the GPU from CUDA entirely by setting `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""` before starting the Ollama server — this forces it to detect no GPU and automatically fall back to CPU-only execution, avoiding the cudaMalloc call altogether. I'll kill the current Ollama process and restart it with this environment variable set.
